     2. A.   12.02.14 
 
 
2.   RECOGNITION  
 
A.   TSD Mission Moment—Smith Connectors 
 
 
The Smith Connectors is a mentoring program that consists of 8 th grade students who 
serve as leaders at Smith Middle School as well as in the community. The Smith 
Connectors are held to a high expectation of leadership, respect, and responsibi lity.  
Their actions and choices both in and out of school  are expected to uphold the 
standards of Smith.  The Connectors are involved in many leadership activities 
throughout the school year such as assisting at Adventure Island, helping 6 th graders 
adjust to middle school, teambuilding and anti-bullying activities.  They do a wonderful 
job of helping to create a positive and safe culture at Smith.  These 8 th grade students 
are role models for the other students at Smith.  The staff at Smith is so proud of each 
one of the Connectors. We would like to invite advisors Jennifer Hartenburg and Ray 
Torres along with the 45 Smith students who serve as Connectors up to receive their 
certificates. 
 
________________________________________________ 
 
Recommended Action:  Invite participants up and present certificates. 
 
 
3
     2. B.   12.02.14 
 
 
2.   RECOGNITION  
 
B.   National Blue Ribbon Award 
 
We are pleased to recognize Wattles Elementary Sch ool and Bemis Elementary 
School tonight for their designation as National Blue Ribbon Schools . This award 
honors them as one of the very best schools in the Unites States.  
 
Secretary Arne Duncan recognized Wattles and Bemis in a ceremony in Washington, D. C. 
last month. Just 337 schools from around the country were chosen for this honor this year. 
This award affirms the hard work of students, staff, parents, and t he Bemis and Wattles 
communities.  As Secretary Duncan stated, “National Blue Ribbon Schools are models of 
consistent excellence and a resource for other schools and districts.  We celebrate them for 
their tireless effort and boundless creativity in reaching and teaching every student.”  
 
Congratulations to both schools for this newest (and impressive) designation! 
________________________________________________ 
 
Recommended Action:  Present certificates.

RECOGNITION 
TSD Mission Moment – 
Leonard Elementary School 
“Peer to Peer Mentoring” 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
NUMATS Award – Tracey 
Kinney 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Last year, Lisa Carruthers, teacher at Leonard 
Elementary School, worked tirelessly to implement the 
“Peer to Peer Mediation” program into her third grade 
classroom.  Peer to Peer Mediation is an evidence-
based practice that positively impacts students with 
Autism Spectrum Disorders as well as other students 
with disabilities. In essence, the Peer to Peer program 
develops peer relationships.  Students learn about 
autism and how it affects other students in their 
classroom.  Mrs. Carruthers attended seminars during 
the summer months and met with other teachers that 
had implemented the program.  Mrs. Carruthers is 
currently busy sharing her knowledge with other 
teachers at Leonard and around the Troy School 
District. Principal, Jerry Cottone, introduced Mrs. 
Carruthers and her student then shared a few 
comments the student and his parents said about Mrs. 
Carruthers. The Board congratulated Mrs. Carruthers 
and presented her with a certificate of recognition. 
  
 
 
Each year, Northwestern University’s Midwest Academic 
Talent Search (NUMATS) recognizes educators from 
across the country for their enthusiastic participation in 
5. A.    12.02.14
6
  2 November 11, 2014 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
ADDITIONS AND DELETIONS 
 
a special program which provides gifted students the 
opportunity to ta ke assessments designed for older 
students. NUMATS, as it is called, is used to determine 
placement in high school courses, and the top scoring 
students are honored in a special ceremony. NUMATS 
also recognizes the important role that educators play 
in the support and implementation of the program. In 
2014, only 18 educators from the United States were 
singled out for extraordinary recognition. The Board 
recognized and congratulated Tracey Kinney, TSD 
Assessment Coordinator, for receiving this prestigious 
recognition.  Ms. Kinney was presented with a 
certificate for her accomplishment.   
 
 
None.

TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT 
TROY, MICHIGAN 48098 
ANNUAL SUMMER TAX RESOLUTION 
 
WHEREAS, this Board of Education by resolution of 
November 11, 2014, determined to impose a summer 
property tax levy to collect one-half of school property 
taxes, including debt services, upon property located 
within the Troy School District, beginning with 2015 
and continuing from year to  year until specifically 
8
  4 November 11, 2014 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
revoked by this Board of Education. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
 
1.  This Board of Education, pursuant to 1976 P.A. 451, 
as amended, hereby invokes a summer tax levy of one-
half of school property taxes, including debt service, 
continuing with 2015, and continuing from year to year 
until specifically revoked by this Board of Education, 
and requests each city in which this district is located 
and in which a local school district or city is 
concurrently imposing a summer tax levy to collect 
those summer taxes.
 
2.  The Superintendent or his/her designee is 
authorized and directed to forward to the governing 
body of each city in which th is district is located and in 
which a local school district or city is concurrently 
imposing a summer tax levy, a copy of this Board’s 
resolution requesting that each such city agree to 
collect the summer tax levy for 2015 in the amount 
specified in this resolution .   S u c h  f o r w a r d i n g  o f  t h e  
resolution and the request to collect the summer tax 
levy shall be sent so that they are received by the 
appropriate governing bodies before January 1, 2015. 
 
 
3.  The Superintendent or his/her designee is 
authorized and directed to negotiate on behalf of this 
District with the governing body of each city in which 
the District is located for the reasonable expenses for 
collection of the District’s summer tax levy that the city 
may bill under MCLA 380.1612.  Any such proposed 
agreement shall be brought before this Board for its 
approval or disapproval.
 
4.  All resolutions and part s of resolutions insofar as 
they conflict with the provisio ns of this resolution be 
and the same are hereby rescinded. 
 
 Yes: Members 
 
 No: Members 
 
Resolution declared adopted.

OTHER 
Bond Projects Update 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Superintendent’s 90-Day Plan 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Ron Curtis from Barton Malow, provided a brief update 
to the Board on the status of bond projects throughout 
the District.  He reported that the Troy High secure 
entrance and auditorium would be completed this 
month as planned.  He reviewed the LGI and multi-use 
classroom renovations at Athens and provided current 
photos indicating that the project would be completed 
by the spring of 2015.  He al so showed photos of the 
upcoming classroom and storage additions at Troy High 
and office addition projects at Boulan and Larson.   
 
There was some discussion among the Board about the 
projects. 
 
 
Dr. Machesky provided a summary report on his 90-day 
plan as the new superintendent of Troy Schools.  The 
main objective of his plan is one team, one plan, and 
one goal to reach every student, every day, in every 
classroom.  He talked about the work he has been 
doing on his goals, objectives, and activities and then 
discussed the outcomes.  He reviewed his goals  as 
follows: 
Goal #1: Student Achievement 
Goal #2: Board Governance

Goal #3: Organizational Alignment 
Goal #4: Community and Public Relations 
Goal #5: Line Support Functions Including 
Human Resources, Finance, Technology and

He mentioned the meetings he  held with building staff, 
teachers, support staff, and administrators.  He also 
met with many constituent groups and spoke to over 15 
community organizations. He and the central office 
administrators attended cu rriculum nights and open 
houses at every school.  He shared that the Board was 
able to participate in a special Breakfast with high 
school students and visit some elementary and 
secondary classrooms recently .  He said that more 
opportunities for the Board to visit with students will be 
scheduled throughout the year.   
 
There was some Board discussion as to the amount of 
work Dr. Machesky put in toward meeting his 90-Day 
Plan and the level of enthusiasm that has been felt 
throughout the District.   
 
 
 
Patty Durell, student representative from Troy High 
School, talked about the girls’ volleyball team playing in 
district finals and the student government fundraisers 
happening.  She announced the Troy High Theatre 
Ensemble performing Romeo & Juliet on November 20, 
21 and 22.   
 
Avi Kumar, student representative from International 
Academy-East shared that they will be holding a Tea 
House, which is a social even t that is held twice year.  
They are also hosting an annual Indian/American 
dinner event for the Diwali holiday.  He also mentioned 
that IA-East students received their Chromebooks on 
Friday, which will allow stud ents to work seamlessly 
with the Bloomfield Google  accounts and allow access 
to school email and the school drive. 
 
Mia Adams, student representative for Niles Community 
High School, shared that five students completed 
graduation requirements and that 18 new student 
joined NCHS.  She added that 20 students registered 
for lasses at Oakland Communi ty College in the Dual 
Enrollment Program. She also mentioned that the 
Women of Tomorrow group hosted a speaker from 
Focus Hope.  She announced the annual Thanksgiving 
Dinner that will be held at the end of the month and 
that the TOTS students will be joining them 
Mr. Hauff mentioned that Troy High would be hosting

the upcoming FIRST Lego League tournament featuring

Mrs. Fleming stated how humbled she felt to be re-
elected to the Board and would be honored to served 
and work with such a wonderful group of people and 
looks forward to the next six years. 
 
Mrs. Edmunds congratulated Mrs. Fleming and Dr. 
Philippart.   
 
Mr. Schmidt talked about the MASB Fall Conference and 
mentioned that he felt the district is operating well 
above our peer districts especially related to the 
teacher evaluation process and that we are leaders in 
that area.   
 
Dr. Machesky shared that he visited Leonard and 
Martell where they celebrated veterans day with “Jeans 
for Troops” and a “GI-GO Fund”.  He mentioned that a 
90-year old World War II veteran was invited to attend.  
He also mentioned elementary and middle school 
conferences would be held on Wednesday and 
Thursday this week. 
 
Dr. Philippart also mentioned how honored and pleased 
she was to be able to serv e on the Board again.  She 
also talked about the grand opening of the Terrace 
Café at Athens High School and invited the public to 
visit for lunch one day.  She also mentioned the MASB 
Fall Conference and talked about needing to advocate 
for public education.  Sh e mentioned the November 
18
th workshop would be held at the Administration 
Building and the regular Board meeting would be held 
on December 2 nd at the Services Building beginning at 
7:00 pm.

District to consider refunding all or a portion of the outstanding 2006 
School Building and Site and Refunding Bonds, dated May 24, 2006 .  At 
current rates, the estimated savings are $3,356,520.  The final savings 
amount will not be known until the final sale of the refunding bonds.

and Refunding Bonds; 
 
BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED that the Troy School District Board of Education

BACKGROUND
1. Act 92 of the Public Acts of Michigan, 2005, as amended, (“Act 92”) enacted pursuant to Article IX, Section 16, of the
Michigan Constitution of 1963, provides the procedure, terms and conditions for the ﬁ nal qualiﬁ cation of bonds for
participation in the School Bond Qualiﬁ cation and Loan Program.
2. This district has taken all necessary actions to comply with all legal and procedural requirements for ﬁ nal qualiﬁ cation
of this bond issue.
ACTION OF THE BOARD
1. The district hereby applies for ﬁ nal qualiﬁ cation of bonds by the State Treasurer for the purpose of:
Financing the school construction and/or
Reﬁ nancing existing debt as described in this application.
2. The bonds of the district quali ﬁ ed by the State Treasurer will conform to all the requirements of law pertaining generally
to school bonds and speciﬁ cally to school bonds qualiﬁ ed under Section 16, Article IX of the 1963 Michigan Constitution, 
Act 92, and Act 112, Public Acts of 1961, as amended.
3. Any moneys obtained through the sale of the quali ﬁ ed bonds of the district as herein proposed will be used only for
the purpose of:
Financing the projects described in the application for preliminary quali ﬁ cation of bonds numbered 
SBL______________________________/and/ or
Reﬁ nancing existing qualiﬁ ed debt and for no other purpose unless such change of purpose is permitted by 
law and has the prior approval of the State Treasurer.
4. The district agrees to annually certify and levy its debt millage tax by ﬁ ling an Annual Loan Activity Statement in
accordance with the requirements of Act 92 and to determine the amounts, if any, to be borrowed from or repaid to the
School Loan Revolving Fund (“SLRF”). The district agrees to deposit proceeds of debt millage upon receipt into an
account established solely for debt service with the appointed banking institution as de ﬁ ned in Section 9. The district
agrees to comply with the provisions of Act 92 governing the periodic recalculation of its millage, the adjustment of its
millage levy when necessary, and the repayment of funds to the SLRF, where applicable.
5. The district agrees to enter into a loan agreement and ﬁ le all necessary applications for qualiﬁ ed loans from the SLRF
along with all supporting information for repayment to the SLRF within statutory application dates and in accordance
with forms and procedures as prescribed by the State Treasurer. The (insert title of authorized school district ofﬁ cial(s))
___________________________________are/is authorized and directed to execute and deliver the loan agreement
and any other documents that may be required by the loan agreement on behalf of the district. The district covenants to
comply with the terms of any applicable qualiﬁ ed loan agreement it is now or may be a party to, including the provisions
related to its millage levy.
6. The district agrees to take actions and refrain from taking actions as necessary to maintain the tax-exempt status of
tax-exempt debt issued by the State of Michigan or the Michigan Finance Authority for the purpose of ﬁ nancing the
School Bond Loan Fund or the School Loan Revolving Fund as deﬁ ned in P.A. 227 of 1985, as amended.
7. The district agrees that if these bonds are issued as tax exempt bonds, it will use the proceeds of these bonds only for
the purposes that are allowed for tax exempt bonds and that none of such proceeds will be used for more than the ﬁ rst
advance refunding of any original bond issued after 1985, nor more than the second advance refunding of any original
bond issued before 1986, and the district further agrees that proceeds of bonds issued as Quali ﬁ ed Zone Academy
Bonds, Qualiﬁ ed School Construction Bonds, Build America Bonds or Recovery Zone Economic Development Bonds[
will only be used for the purposes that are allowed for such bonds.
8. The district agrees to use any funds borrowed from the SLRF only for the payment of principal and interest on quali ﬁ ed
bonds that is immediately payable to bondholders and not to fund escrow or sinking funds.
157
Form 3451 Page 3
9. The district agrees to appoint a banking institution that performs paying agent services in general, and to execute a 
signed agreement that provides, at a minimum, the following procedures:
a. If the district has not established an irrevocable escrow account with a quali ﬁ ed escrow agent, the district agrees 
to submit debt service payments for its qualiﬁ ed bonds in immediately available funds to its banking institution no 
less than ﬁ ve business days prior to the debt service due date, and agrees not to withdraw, or cause a debit to 
be drawn against, such funds except to pay debt service.
b. The district agrees to use an existing or establish a new interest bearing, money market or investment account 
with the banking institution that performs paying agent services for the subject bonds, that allows the district to 
provide written investment instructions for the investment of collected funds on hand preceding the debt service 
due date.
c. The paying agent will implement noti ﬁ cation procedures that provide that if suf ﬁ cient funds for full payment 
of debt service do not reach the banking institution ﬁ ve business days prior to the debt service payment due 
date, the paying agent will notify the district of the amount of insuf ﬁ cient funds four business days prior to the 
debt service payment due date. In the event that the district does not immediately resolve the insuf ﬁ cient funds 
situation, the paying agent will notify the Michigan Department of Treasury of the delinquency three business 
days before the payment due date.
d. The district agrees to furnish written noti ﬁ cation to the paying agent and the Department of Treasury of any bonds 
that have been refunded.
10. The district agrees to deposit all federal interest credits received with respect to its quali ﬁ ed bonds issued as direct 
credit type bonds into the debt retirement fund payable for such bonds.
11. The district requests that the State Treasurer increase its computed millage if at any time the full amount of any tax 
credit related to direct credit type bonds is not received or the amount of debt service on its qualiﬁ ed bonds increases 
for any reason and the current computed millage is not suf ﬁ cient to repay all outstanding quali ﬁ ed loans by the ﬁ nal 
mandatory repayment date.
12. The district agrees that if Treasury determines that the district will not be able to make all or part of the debt service 
payment, Treasury will process an emergency loan from the SLRF. If the district incurs an emergency loan it shall be a 
legal debt of the district and the State Treasurer shall bill the school district for the amount paid and the school district 
shall remit the amount to the state.
13. The board directs the school district administration to report any failure to perform as a result of this application. In 
the event that the district fails to perform any actions as identi ﬁ ed in this application or required by law, the district 
will submit to the State Treasurer a board approved resolution which indicates the actions taken and procedures 
implemented to assure future compliance.
14. The district board members have read this application, approved all statements and representations contained herein 
as true to the best knowledge and belief of said board, and authorized the Secretary of the Board of Education to sign 
this Final Application and submit same to the State Treasurer for his or her review and approval.
Ayes: Members _______________________________________________________________________________
Nays: Members _______________________________________________________________________________

 F.   INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY ARCHITECT BID RETRACTION 
 
 On May 7, 2013 , the Board of Education authorized administration to 
proceed with finalizing a contract with Integrated Design Solutions, LLC 
(IDS) to provide  information technology consultant services for the 2014 
bond projects.  Subsequent to this award, it was determined that IDS did 
not align with the  District’s strategic vision for technology needs and the 
contract was never finalized .  It is recommended the bid be rescinded as 
stated below. 
 
 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 
 
 Moved by:   
 
 Supported by: 
 
 
 Be it therefore resolved that the award for information technology consultant 
services to Integrated Design Solutions, LLC be rescinded.

awarded to Gorno Ford in the amount of $28,479.00 and that this amount 
be approved as an expenditure of the 2014-2015 General Fund.

TO:  Rich Machesky - Superintendent  
 
FROM:  Rob Carson – Director of Maintenance & Operations   
           
RE:  Fleet Vehicle Purchase – State Contract Pricing 
 
DATE:  November 25, 2014  
 
 
 
 
A new vehicle purchase is requested to supplement our existing Operations Fleet for 
the purpose of snowplow ing and grounds services.  This vehicle will specifically be 
assigned to all of the new bus and drop off loops and other additional paved surfaces 
recently completed as part of the Bond Construction Projects.  This vehicle will assist 
the existing fleet which is primarily made up of dump trucks that plow and salt all 
large open parking areas. 
 
Pricing was obtained from Gorno Ford utilizing the M iDEAL State Contract Pricing 
(Contract # 071B1300005) available to schools and municipalities. The specific 
vehicle is a 2015 Ford F-250 4X4 Truck with plow prep package and municipal safety 
lighting to meet all regulatory requirements.  The total cost requested for purchase is 
$28,479.00.  The funding for this purchase will be from the 2014-2015 General Fund. 
 
If you have any questions, let me know.  
 
 
 
 
Cc:   Pat Orczykowski – Business Department
